# Unlock 2 · Expanded Writing Studio · Edition 2

Twelve individual 90-minute classes, four in each unit. Every lesson now has 32 timed core slides, with substantial learner tasks, two-part model study, independent writing, feedback and revision. The course has 384 core slides and 64 optional extension slides (448 total). Optional slides sit outside the 90-minute sequence.

## Open and teach

Extract the ZIP and open index.html. Each lesson is self-contained, with inline styles/scripts and embedded textbook pages. Keep the files together for library/worksheet links. All classroom features work offline; reference URLs and licensed publisher videos require separate access.

Use arrows or Page Up/Down; A reveals the model, N opens notes, W opens Writing Desk, T opens the complete assignment. Shortcuts pause while typing and while dialogs are open. Escape closes a dialog. Use the slide menu to jump. TTD workshop → goes straight to the actual workshop in the main sequence; it does not merely open a brief.

## The two writing spaces

The lesson work pad keeps short activity responses separately for each class. Use Write / keep response on a slide, Show all lesson tasks to review, Export responses to keep text and Print responses for paper. The Writing Desk keeps the full paragraph, source/plan, revision receipts, visual credit and teaching plan for one two-lesson assignment. Its existing paired storage keys and JSON format are preserved, so previous writing remains compatible. Import/Export JSON to carry work between lessons/devices; export text for a readable packet. Browser saving is attempted and failures are reported. Files do not send writing to a server. The printable lesson worksheets are an alternative to typing every response.

## Pacing and writing focus

Odd lessons give 14 uninterrupted minutes to the first full draft; even lessons give 12 uninterrupted minutes to substantive revision. Directed sentence writing, planning, model analysis, feedback decisions and two revision blocks surround that studio time. The schedule totals 90 minutes continuously; it does not assume that optional activities fill the gap. Do not interrupt the studio for a whole-class correction. Activity checkpoints can stay open during the sprint.

Textbook tasks specify selected items when the whole exercise would exceed the time. Choose the named number of items before class. For learners needing more processing time, use fewer selected reading/language items and more oral rehearsal; protect the full paragraph-writing and teaching blocks. Use extensions for early finishers, homework or a later class. Real classroom pace still depends on the group; review the named products rather than advancing solely because a timer finishes.

## Six Teach the Document assignments

Each odd lesson (1 and3 in every unit) now has a five-screen, ten-minute launch at25–35 minutes: task → visible document → annotation → written start → packet requirements. It is part of the main slideshow and is linked from both the cover and toolbar. All six also have separate printable workshop HTML sheets. Each even lesson has full paired teaching at74–84 minutes: A teaches4 minutes; B gives1 minute feedback; B teaches4 minutes; A gives1 minute feedback. All pairs work simultaneously, so the timing works for a full class. Odd-lesson micro-teaching is a shorter rehearsal, not the final3–5-minute task.

Final work includes the written packet and a3–5-minute English explanation/recording or teacher-directed live presentation. Use the announced platform/date; none is invented. Assess the assignment’s five rubric areas independently,4 points each. Checkboxes assess completeness, not quality.

## Source alignment and evidence

Embedded printed ranges remain complete: Unit2 pp.36–57; Unit3 pp.58–79; Unit4 pp.80–101, with contents pp.4–5 in later units. Unit2 came from the supplied HTML; Units3–4 came from the two readable textbook excerpts. The former blank-page gap remains resolved. The source attachments are unchanged. Original Unit2 Lesson1 information remains under Teacher notes → Original lesson information (42 original slides, Writing Desk material and page guidance;70 reference sections). Its16 original optional extensions also remain. The revised main sequence has32 core slides.

Teacher-created preparation tasks are distinguished from the official textbook final tasks. Unit3’s official p.77 opinion task requires THREE supporting ideas. Unit4’s official task compares two places and both temperature/precipitation. The privacy task and one-place description prepare those skills.

Festival accounts are textbook/source summaries, not verified current event schedules. Do not import festival facts between texts; mark food/clothing gaps. The p.63 article is reading evidence, not current legal guidance. The p.67 UK games statistic has population/topic/time limits and is not a current global internet statistic. Mira/Leon sessions are explicitly fictional illustrations, not measured research.

Weather: identify place, month, measure, series and unit. Average highs/lows are not records. Use about for estimates, and label calculations. Printed p.93 examples: January average highs Amman13°C/Svalbard−13°C (calculated26°C difference); September precipitation Amman0mm/Svalbard25mm (calculated25mm difference); Amman July high31°C, February precipitation61mm, November28mm. No calendar year, tomorrow forecast, causal explanation or long-term climate trend is invented. The Sahara daily graph is a different time scale/source; the survival passage is analyzed as a text, not independently validated practical advice.

Publisher media remain separate. The labeled offline reading routes are complete and do not claim video-only answers. Use a licensed short excerpt only within the selected activity timing.

## Unit 2 · Lesson 1 · Festival Field Guide: Plan

Design: **Festival Passport** — Travel notebook · route lines · source stamps.

| Minutes | Activity | Product |
|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | A festival is a place.Give it a voice. | A five-part festival map, a first descriptive paragraph and a started TTD #1 packet. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | I can name a specific festival, organize five kinds of detail, write complete sentences and explain where my evidence comes from. |
| 02–05 | Start with something you can describe. | A two-sentence baseline to compare with the final draft. |
| 05–07 | Notice / infer / verify. | Three labeled notes: N / I / V. |
| 07–10 | A word needs a usable context. | Three checked meanings + one usable phrase. |
| 10–14 | Read the headings before the article. | A set of short section labels, not a translation. |
| 14–18 | Which detail answers which question? | Three notes with their source locations. |
| 18–21 | A source has boundaries. | One supported claim and one cautious revision. |
| 21–23 | A map gives the details a home. | Two classifications and a reason. |
| 23–25 | Try a map-to-sentence move. | One original sentence based on source notes. |
| 25–27 | Teach the Document #1 · START HERE | By the end of this pair: a five-part map, 7+ original sentences, an explained revision, 1–2 credited visuals and a 3–5-minute English lesson. |
| 27–29 | Your document is on the table. | A location, two evidence details and a genuine research gap. |
| 29–31 | Annotate. Locate. Explain. | Five labeled notes, including an honest gap. |
| 31–34 | Begin the assignment in class. | A festival passport and a first topic sentence. |
| 34–35 | What must your packet contain? | Circle what is already started. Put a star beside the next action for Lesson 2. |
| 35–37 | A sentence needs a complete core. | A subject + verb marked in a complete sentence. |
| 37–40 | Make the noun and action precise. | Two precise noun/action choices and one complete sentence. |
| 40–43 | Add context without losing the sentence. | Two grammatical versions with the same meaning. |
| 43–45 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 45–47 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 47–50 | Plan the jobs before the sentences. | A five-part map and a 7+ sentence route. |
| 50–52 | A partner tests the plan. | One precise suggestion and one decision recorded by the writer. |
| 52–53 | Set up a quiet writing sprint. | A visible plan, source, and a blank paragraph space. |
| 53–67 | Writing studio · 14 uninterrupted minutes. | A 7+ sentence first descriptive draft + visible evidence gaps. |
| 67–68 | Peer review · agree on the reader’s job. | Partner names + the part each reader will verify. |
| 68–73 | Peer conference · evidence before advice. | One “keep” comment and one actionable “revise” comment per writer. |
| 73–77 | Revision 1 · improve the meaning. | Before → after → why, with an improved sentence in the paragraph. |
| 77–80 | Revision 2 · make the language accurate. | An edited sentence + a reason connected to this lesson’s language target. |
| 80–82 | Build the document you will point to. | A labeled visual draft with a source credit. |
| 82–85 | Teach a small part before the full talk. | Each partner gives a 60-second document explanation and receives 30 seconds of feedback. |
| 85–88 | Exit ticket · show the writing skill. | Three short responses: evidence, writing choice, next step. |
| 88–90 | Save today. Arrive ready next time. | Exported writing + a named action for the next lesson. |

Stretch: justify a choice, improve precision, or explain a source limit; do not add unsupported claims.


**33 · Model 01 · The complete submission packet · optional, outside90 minutes**

Stress academic integrity: students may study the model but must make their own research, paragraph, visuals, and video.


**34 · Model 02 · Where did these facts come from? · optional, outside90 minutes**

Source 1 is organizer, source 2 is Japan National Tourism Organization. Show the exact URLs; the HTML works offline but these URLs require a connection if followed. Avoid claims about visitors’ food without a separate citation.

Answer/model: A source supports a claim only if it actually says it. A pretty photo does not prove festival dates.Food is a documented research gap, not a reason to fabricate a dish.


**35 · Model 03 · A finished five-part ideas map · optional, outside90 minutes**

This map is complete as a planning document. Research gap in food/drinks remains clearly visible. If teacher requires a verified food example later, students must find additional credible evidence.

Answer/model: When and where are straightforward supported facts.The food branch is honestly marked as unverified in the chosen sources.Do not claim all spectators wear haneto costumes.


**36 · Model 04 · A complete nine-sentence paragraph · optional, outside90 minutes**

Give students one minute to locate topic, support and concluding sentences before showing explanations. These are original teacher-created sentences, not Cambridge textbook text.

Answer/model: The first sentence establishes the festival and its location.The seven middle sentences support the topic with verified details.The final sentence summarizes what makes the event distinctive.


**37 · Model 05 · Prove that you revised your writing · optional, outside90 minutes**

A correction without a reason is only partial evidence of revision. Students need the before, after, and explanation.

Answer/model: The improved sentence makes the noun and action specific.The student’s explanation must identify what improved and why.


**38 · Model 06 · Two visuals with real source credits · optional, outside90 minutes**

These are teacher-made sample infographic visuals, not photographs of a particular event. The captions illustrate how to credit a student-created visual and identify the source of facts.


**39 · Model 07 · A four-minute presentation plan · optional, outside90 minutes**

The complete spoken script appears in the writing workspace. It is a demonstration, not a text for copying verbatim. Students should use their own festival research.


**40 · Model 08 · Check the sample against the rubric · optional, outside90 minutes**

Model success indicators are guidance rather than a teacher marking actual student performance. The script and infographics are example artifacts.

Answer/model: Check source reliability and accurate location.Check map, 7+ sentence paragraph and revision explanation.Check visual credits and 3–5-minute timing.


**41 · Extra: Is this a fact or an inference? · optional, outside90 minutes**

Optional challenge. Use only if core work is complete.

Answer/model: Visible actions/objects can be described directly if shown.Claiming a national holiday requires a credible date/context source.


**42 · Extra: Improve three vague sentences. · optional, outside90 minutes**

Students write factual alternatives appropriate to their chosen event; do not prescribe unsupported details.

Answer/model: Example only: “Participants watch a parade and share a meal.”Example only: “The event takes place in the main square in the evening.”Example only: “Some families prepare a traditional dish together.”


**43 · Extra: Read the real article later. · optional, outside90 minutes**

Reading 1 detailed comprehension belongs to Lesson 2. Use this as optional preview.


**44 · Extra: Topic, support, or conclusion? · optional, outside90 minutes**

Students label T/S/C from teacher-created short model.

Answer/model: A = Topic sentenceB = Supporting sentenceC = Concluding sentence


**45 · Extra: Correct the paragraph order. · optional, outside90 minutes**

The correct sequence is 3,2,4,1. Supports paragraph structure p52 without spoiling complete unit writing task.

Answer/model: 3 → 2 → 4 → 1: introduction, details, additional detail, conclusion


**46 · Extra: Spot a risky cultural generalization. · optional, outside90 minutes**

Distinguish observed experience from national generalization; no need to ask about religious affiliation.

Answer/model: The statement “Everyone in the country celebrates” is not supported by one festival photograph or a single local event.A safer sourced sentence names a specific city and a visible practice: “The official festival website describes illuminated float parades in Aomori, Japan.”


**47 · Extra: Preview Unit 2 writing task. · optional, outside90 minutes**

Explicitly reserve full p54 fill-in and editing p55/56 for later unit lessons. Do not turn the preview into a complete re-teach.


**48 · Extra: Personal conference. · optional, outside90 minutes**

Use in longer lessons or students who finish early.


**49 · Source detective · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Compare two sources for your festival. Identify one detail they both establish and one difference in scope.

Product: A two-source comparison with a research question.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A two-source comparison with a research question.


**50 · Alternative audience · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Rewrite your opening for a visitor who has never heard of the festival. Explain what you added.

Product: Two openings and an audience-based explanation.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: Two openings and an audience-based explanation.


**51 · Sensory detail test · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Add one sensory detail only if a credited image/text supports it. Explain what remains an inference.

Product: A supported detail and an evidence label.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A supported detail and an evidence label.


**52 · Paragraph rescue · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Write a deliberately vague two-sentence description. Exchange and revise it with precise supported nouns/actions.

Product: Before/after sentences and reasons.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: Before/after sentences and reasons.

## Unit 2 · Lesson 3 · Festival Editor: Read and Select

Design: **The Festival Desk** — Newspaper masthead · red rules · proof sheets.

| Minutes | Activity | Product |
|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | Every detailneeds an editor. | An annotated Muscat source, five-part organizer and 8–10-sentence first draft for TTD #2. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | Leave with a written product, source evidence, a meaningful revision and a clear next step. |
| 02–05 | Your first sentence needs a job. | One labeled topic sentence. |
| 05–07 | The editor’s red pen. | A bounded replacement. |
| 07–10 | Six tools; three useful choices. | Three meanings + one phrase. |
| 10–14 | Skim Muscat for paragraph jobs. | Five paragraph labels. |
| 14–18 | Read for detail, then show the proof. | Two source-linked answers. |
| 18–21 | What does a visitor still need to ask? | An answered question or a clearly marked information gap. |
| 21–23 | General words do not name a dish. | One source-faithful food note. |
| 23–25 | Build a source-framed sentence. | One accurate source-framed sentence. |
| 25–27 | Teach the Document #2 · START HERE | An annotated p.44 source, five-part map, 8–10 original sentences, two explained revisions, a credited organizer and a 3–5-minute explanation. |
| 27–29 | Your document is on the table. | Two located details with paragraph numbers. |
| 29–31 | Annotate. Locate. Explain. | Three annotations: fact, activity evidence, limitation. |
| 31–34 | Begin the assignment in class. | The beginning of your own editor’s organizer and paragraph. |
| 34–35 | What must your packet contain? | Circle what is already started. Put a star beside the next action for Lesson 4. |
| 35–37 | Who does what? | A labeled sentence core + an expanded version. |
| 37–40 | Time, place or manner? | Two classified phrases + one complete sentence. |
| 40–43 | Move context; preserve meaning. | Two versions + an editorial choice. |
| 43–45 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 45–47 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 47–50 | Plan the jobs before the sentences. | Five-part map + numbered 8–10-sentence plan. |
| 50–52 | A partner tests the plan. | One precise suggestion and one decision recorded by the writer. |
| 52–53 | Set up a quiet writing sprint. | A visible plan, source, and a blank paragraph space. |
| 53–67 | Writing studio · 14 uninterrupted minutes. | An 8–10-sentence first draft with p.44 evidence. |
| 67–68 | Peer review · agree on the reader’s job. | Partner names + the part each reader will verify. |
| 68–73 | Peer conference · evidence before advice. | One “keep” comment and one actionable “revise” comment per writer. |
| 73–77 | Revision 1 · improve the meaning. | Before → after → why, with an improved sentence in the paragraph. |
| 77–80 | Revision 2 · make the language accurate. | An edited sentence + a reason connected to this lesson’s language target. |
| 80–82 | Build the document you will point to. | A labeled visual draft with a source credit. |
| 82–85 | Teach a small part before the full talk. | Each partner gives a 60-second document explanation and receives 30 seconds of feedback. |
| 85–88 | Exit ticket · show the writing skill. | Three short responses: evidence, writing choice, next step. |
| 88–90 | Save today. Arrive ready next time. | Exported writing + a named action for the next lesson. |

Task: Turn an unsupported food/clothes assumption into a research question.

Product: A question and a suitable source type.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A question and a suitable source type.


**36 · Sentence expansion ladder · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Expand a subject–verb core three times without adding unsupported facts.

Product: A three-step sentence ladder.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A three-step sentence ladder.


## Unit 2 · Lesson 4 · Festival Editor: Revise and Publish

Design: **Culture Zine** — Lilac cut-paper · bold outlines · playful type.

| Minutes | Activity | Product |
|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | Cut. Move. Rewrite.Publish with care. | A coherent Muscat paragraph, two explained revisions and a full TTD #2 teaching rehearsal. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | Leave with a written product, source evidence, a meaningful revision and a clear next step. |
| 02–05 | Read your draft with the title covered. | A tested opening sentence. |
| 05–07 | The paragraph is a reader’s route. | One organization problem to investigate. |
| 07–10 | Topic / support / conclusion. | Three role labels + a reason. |
| 10–15 | Holi · give the sentences a sequence. | A checked sequence and two linking clues. |
| 15–18 | Las Fallas · map to model. | Two map-to-sentence links. |
| 18–21 | Transfer the move, keep the sources apart. | One revised sentence sequence in your own plan. |
| 21–23 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 23–25 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 25–28 | Join the ideas, not just the words. | One complete supported sentence. |
| 28–31 | Find the misplaced sentence. | A sentence move with a reader-centered reason. |
| 31–34 | Use the book’s language checklist. | Two checked language choices. |
| 34–36 | Teach the Document #2 · reconnect. | A packet with the document, draft and revision evidence open. |
| 36–38 | Use the rubric as a revision tool. | One priority criterion and one sentence explaining the needed improvement. |
| 38–41 | Choose two changes before rewriting. | One meaning/evidence goal + one language goal. |
| 41–53 | Writing studio · 12 uninterrupted minutes. | A coherent final descriptive paragraph with source-faithful details. |
| 53–55 | Read it as someone new to the topic. | Two underlined strengths and one circled problem to discuss. |
| 55–56 | Before you exchange the paragraph… | A partner and a specific question for the reader. |
| 56–61 | Five-minute peer conference. | One located problem, one suggested repair and the writer’s decision. |
| 61–65 | Revise the content before polishing. | A meaningful before → after → reason revision receipt. |
| 65–68 | Edit the language that carries the meaning. | The final language revision receipt + corrected paragraph. |
| 68–70 | A visual should help a listener read. | A credited document with readable labels and selected annotations. |
| 70–73 | Build a 4-minute teaching route. | A cue outline, not a paragraph read aloud without explanation. |
| 73–74 | Partners A and B · set the stage. | Document visible, listener question ready, roles assigned. |
| 74–78 | TTD #2 · Partner A teaches. | A 4-minute explanation with a document-based listener check. |
| 78–79 | Listener B · one minute of feedback. | One accurate observation + one next action for A. |
| 79–83 | TTD #2 · Partner B teaches. | B’s 4-minute explanation; A answers the source question. |
| 83–84 | Listener A · one minute of feedback. | One accurate observation + one next action for B. |
| 84–86 | Make the rehearsal improve the writing. | One final change based on the listener’s response. |
| 86–88 | Exit ticket · prove the skill. | A short written self-assessment with evidence from your paragraph. |
| 88–90 | Check the packet. Export. Keep it. | A final packet and an exported editable copy. |

Product: A short portfolio annotation.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A short portfolio annotation.


## Unit 3 · Lesson 1 · Digital Privacy: Read and Plan

Design: **Privacy Lab** — Green terminal · dark dashboard · evidence logs.

| Minutes | Activity | Product |
|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | Your data.Your choices.Your argument. | A source/evidence/inference table, a privacy-control position and an 8–10-sentence first draft for TTD #3. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | I can separate source statements from my own view, develop at least two reasons, and qualify an unsupported claim. |
| 02–05 | A new purpose for the same paragraph skills. | Two sentences labeled description / opinion. |
| 05–07 | A targeted advert is a clue. | One cautious inference and one limit. |
| 07–10 | Vocabulary as a process. | Three checked meanings and a process phrase. |
| 10–14 | Read for the article’s paragraph jobs. | Paragraph labels + one evidence-based choice. |
| 14–18 | Locate two statements before arguing. | Two source statements with references. |
| 18–21 | Source / inference / unsupported. | Three classifications + one explanation. |
| 21–23 | The reason is yours to explain. | An explanation linking evidence to a reason. |
| 23–25 | Try a cautious source sentence. | Two sentences that separate source and interpretation. |
| 25–27 | Teach the Document #3 · START HERE | A p.63 evidence table, one clear position, an 8–10-sentence opinion paragraph, two revisions, an annotated visual and a 3–5-minute lesson. |
| 27–29 | Your document is on the table. | Two paragraph references and one limit to the source. |
| 29–31 | Annotate. Locate. Explain. | S / S / I / L evidence notes with source locations. |
| 31–34 | Begin the assignment in class. | A position + two reason/evidence/explanation chains. |
| 34–35 | What must your packet contain? | Circle what is already started. Put a star beside the next action for Lesson 2. |
| 35–37 | The position should be easy to find. | One clear opinion topic sentence. |
| 37–40 | Because should explain a reason. | A reason + because-clause with added meaning. |
| 40–43 | Qualify the size of the claim. | A defensible source statement + one self-edit. |
| 43–45 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 45–47 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 47–50 | Plan the jobs before the sentences. | Two or more reason/evidence/explanation chains and a consistent ending. |
| 50–52 | A partner tests the plan. | One precise suggestion and one decision recorded by the writer. |
| 52–53 | Set up a quiet writing sprint. | A visible plan, source, and a blank paragraph space. |
| 53–67 | Writing studio · 14 uninterrupted minutes. | An 8–10-sentence privacy-control draft with scoped source use. |
| 67–68 | Peer review · agree on the reader’s job. | Partner names + the part each reader will verify. |
| 68–73 | Peer conference · evidence before advice. | One “keep” comment and one actionable “revise” comment per writer. |
| 73–77 | Revision 1 · improve the meaning. | Before → after → why, with an improved sentence in the paragraph. |
| 77–80 | Revision 2 · make the language accurate. | An edited sentence + a reason connected to this lesson’s language target. |
| 80–82 | Build the document you will point to. | A labeled visual draft with a source credit. |
| 82–85 | Teach a small part before the full talk. | Each partner gives a 60-second document explanation and receives 30 seconds of feedback. |
| 85–88 | Exit ticket · show the writing skill. | Three short responses: evidence, writing choice, next step. |
| 88–90 | Save today. Arrive ready next time. | Exported writing + a named action for the next lesson. |

**33 · Inference ladder · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Write a source statement, cautious inference and unsupported leap about one advert.

Product: Three evidence-status labels.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: Three evidence-status labels.


**34 · Evidence alternatives · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Find a different p.63 point that supports your chosen reason. Compare its usefulness.

Product: An evidence selection explanation.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: An evidence selection explanation.


**35 · Counter-question · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Write a skeptical reader’s question and answer it without overstating the source.

Product: A question/answer pair.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A question/answer pair.


**36 · Position scope · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Rewrite an absolute opinion as a qualified but clear position.

Product: Two versions and a scope explanation.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: Two versions and a scope explanation.


## Unit 3 · Lesson 2 · Digital Privacy: Develop and Teach

Design: **Signal & Sentence** — Blue systems map · connected nodes · open space.

| Minutes | Activity | Product |
|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | Make every connectioncarry meaning. | A developed privacy opinion, two explained revisions and a full TTD #3 teaching rehearsal. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | Leave with a written product, source evidence, a meaningful revision and a clear next step. |
| 02–05 | Retrieve the whole reasoning chain. | A checked position → reason → evidence → explanation chain. |
| 05–07 | A connector cannot invent logic. | One reason the connection is irrelevant. |
| 07–10 | Compound nouns need clear meanings. | Three meanings + one complete sentence. |
| 10–15 | Advertising · follow the evidence route. | A labeled source route and three evidence notes. |
| 15–18 | Does this example belong? | A relevant/irrelevant decision with a reason. |
| 18–21 | Develop a reason beyond a label. | A two-sentence development of one reason. |
| 21–23 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 23–25 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 25–28 | and / also / too · placement matters. | Two accurate addition sentences. |
| 28–31 | Two clauses need a real join. | A corrected compound sentence. |
| 31–34 | However needs a sentence boundary. | Two complete contrast versions. |
| 34–36 | Teach the Document #3 · reconnect. | A packet with the document, draft and revision evidence open. |
| 36–38 | Use the rubric as a revision tool. | One priority criterion and one sentence explaining the needed improvement. |
| 38–41 | Choose two changes before rewriting. | One meaning/evidence goal + one language goal. |
| 41–53 | Writing studio · 12 uninterrupted minutes. | A developed privacy paragraph with accurate sentence connections. |
| 53–55 | Read it as someone new to the topic. | Two underlined strengths and one circled problem to discuss. |
| 55–56 | Before you exchange the paragraph… | A partner and a specific question for the reader. |
| 56–61 | Five-minute peer conference. | One located problem, one suggested repair and the writer’s decision. |
| 61–65 | Revise the content before polishing. | A meaningful before → after → reason revision receipt. |
| 65–68 | Edit the language that carries the meaning. | The final language revision receipt + corrected paragraph. |
| 68–70 | A visual should help a listener read. | A credited document with readable labels and selected annotations. |
| 70–73 | Build a 4-minute teaching route. | A cue outline, not a paragraph read aloud without explanation. |
| 73–74 | Partners A and B · set the stage. | Document visible, listener question ready, roles assigned. |
| 74–78 | TTD #3 · Partner A teaches. | A 4-minute explanation with a document-based listener check. |
| 78–79 | Listener B · one minute of feedback. | One accurate observation + one next action for A. |
| 79–83 | TTD #3 · Partner B teaches. | B’s 4-minute explanation; A answers the source question. |
| 83–84 | Listener A · one minute of feedback. | One accurate observation + one next action for B. |
| 84–86 | Make the rehearsal improve the writing. | One final change based on the listener’s response. |
| 86–88 | Exit ticket · prove the skill. | A short written self-assessment with evidence from your paragraph. |
| 88–90 | Check the packet. Export. Keep it. | A final packet and an exported editable copy. |

## Unit 3 · Lesson 3 · Internet Time: Choose and Draft

Design: **The Opinion Arena** — Burgundy debate hall · divided arguments · speech forms.

| Minutes | Activity | Product |
|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | One question.Three reasons.Your verdict. | A p.77 stance, THREE developed supporting ideas and an 8–10-sentence first draft for TTD #4. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | I can interpret the official question, select three relevant ideas and keep a consistent opinion across the paragraph. |
| 02–05 | Keep the method; change the question. | A transferable writing move. |
| 05–07 | Quick verdict · choose a side. | A conditional opinion that distinguishes purposeful work from distraction. |
| 07–10 | Benefits and drawbacks need verbs. | Three meanings and two short phrases. |
| 10–14 | Read both sides before choosing yours. | Paragraph labels and one supported point from each side. |
| 14–18 | Stated or just plausible? | Four source-checked decisions. |
| 18–21 | A statistic has a population. | A scoped evidence note and a limit. |
| 21–23 | Read the task, not just the topic. | An accurate statement of the required response. |
| 23–25 | Select relevant ideas. | A relevance decision with an explanation. |
| 25–27 | Teach the Document #4 · START HERE | An official p.77 opinion paragraph: one position, THREE supporting ideas, 8–10 sentences, two revisions, a relevant annotated document and a 3–5-minute lesson. |
| 27–29 | Your document is on the table. | An exact task interpretation + one useful illustration. |
| 29–31 | Annotate. Locate. Explain. | Two annotations + one limitation. |
| 31–34 | Begin the assignment in class. | A stance and three developed points, ready for paragraph writing. |
| 34–35 | What must your packet contain? | Circle what is already started. Put a star beside the next action for Lesson 4. |
| 35–37 | The opening must answer the question. | A task-focused opinion topic sentence. |
| 37–40 | Three ideas need different jobs. | Three non-duplicate supporting ideas. |
| 40–43 | Explain the illustration’s role. | A developed support chain with honest source framing. |
| 43–45 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 45–47 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 47–50 | Plan the jobs before the sentences. | A three-idea paragraph plan, with evidence status visible. |
| 50–52 | A partner tests the plan. | One precise suggestion and one decision recorded by the writer. |
| 52–53 | Set up a quiet writing sprint. | A visible plan, source, and a blank paragraph space. |
| 53–67 | Writing studio · 14 uninterrupted minutes. | An 8–10-sentence internet-time draft with three supporting ideas. |
| 67–68 | Peer review · agree on the reader’s job. | Partner names + the part each reader will verify. |
| 68–73 | Peer conference · evidence before advice. | One “keep” comment and one actionable “revise” comment per writer. |
| 73–77 | Revision 1 · improve the meaning. | Before → after → why, with an improved sentence in the paragraph. |
| 77–80 | Revision 2 · make the language accurate. | An edited sentence + a reason connected to this lesson’s language target. |
| 80–82 | Build the document you will point to. | A labeled visual draft with a source credit. |
| 82–85 | Teach a small part before the full talk. | Each partner gives a 60-second document explanation and receives 30 seconds of feedback. |
| 85–88 | Exit ticket · show the writing skill. | Three short responses: evidence, writing choice, next step. |
| 88–90 | Save today. Arrive ready next time. | Exported writing + a named action for the next lesson. |

**24 · Writing studio · 14 uninterrupted minutes.**

First, ___ because ___. For example, ___. This matters because ___. For these reasons, ___. Stretch: justify a choice, improve precision, or explain a source limit; do not add unsupported claims.

**33 · Argue the other side · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Plan three supports for the opposite position, then explain why your original stance remains defensible.

Product: An alternative plan, kept separate from the final paragraph.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: An alternative plan, kept separate from the final paragraph.


**34 · Duplicate-reason test · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Try to collapse two reasons into one. If possible, develop a genuinely different third support.

Product: A non-duplicate three-idea plan.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A non-duplicate three-idea plan.


**35 · Fiction versus data · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Rewrite an invented example as an honest illustration; explain what research would be needed for a population claim.

Product: Two evidence-status notes.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: Two evidence-status notes.


**36 · Prompt drift detector · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Write a plausible but off-topic sentence. Explain why the official question does not need it.

Product: A relevance explanation.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A relevance explanation.


## Unit 3 · Lesson 4 · Internet Time: Revise and Publish

Design: **The Opinion Press** — Black-and-white publishing · cobalt marginalia · numbered proofs.

| Minutes | Activity | Product |
|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | A stronger argumentis an edited argument. | A polished p.77 opinion with three supports, two explained revisions and a full TTD #4 teaching rehearsal. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | Leave with a written product, source evidence, a meaningful revision and a clear next step. |
| 02–05 | Say the answer before the reasons. | A compact argument map. |
| 05–07 | A shiny number can distract a writer. | One evidence-check question. |
| 07–10 | Find the controlling idea. | One choice with a reason. |
| 10–15 | Source audit · what are you really using? | Three evidence-status labels + one scope limit. |
| 15–18 | A paragraph has a logical sequence. | An ordered chain + a reminder of all three ideas. |
| 18–21 | Use the actual checklist. | Two diagnosed issues with quoted evidence. |
| 21–23 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 23–25 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 25–28 | Connect ideas without a comma splice. | A corrected join and one self-check. |
| 28–31 | A qualification keeps the main position. | A qualified opinion with a complete sentence boundary. |
| 31–34 | The ending must keep its promise. | A consistent concluding sentence. |
| 34–36 | Teach the Document #4 · reconnect. | A packet with the document, draft and revision evidence open. |
| 36–38 | Use the rubric as a revision tool. | One priority criterion and one sentence explaining the needed improvement. |
| 38–41 | Choose two changes before rewriting. | One meaning/evidence goal + one language goal. |
| 41–53 | Writing studio · 12 uninterrupted minutes. | A final opinion paragraph meeting the p.77–78 requirements. |
| 53–55 | Read it as someone new to the topic. | Two underlined strengths and one circled problem to discuss. |
| 55–56 | Before you exchange the paragraph… | A partner and a specific question for the reader. |
| 56–61 | Five-minute peer conference. | One located problem, one suggested repair and the writer’s decision. |
| 61–65 | Revise the content before polishing. | A meaningful before → after → reason revision receipt. |
| 65–68 | Edit the language that carries the meaning. | The final language revision receipt + corrected paragraph. |
| 68–70 | A visual should help a listener read. | A credited document with readable labels and selected annotations. |
| 70–73 | Build a 4-minute teaching route. | A cue outline, not a paragraph read aloud without explanation. |
| 73–74 | Partners A and B · set the stage. | Document visible, listener question ready, roles assigned. |
| 74–78 | TTD #4 · Partner A teaches. | A 4-minute explanation with a document-based listener check. |
| 78–79 | Listener B · one minute of feedback. | One accurate observation + one next action for A. |
| 79–83 | TTD #4 · Partner B teaches. | B’s 4-minute explanation; A answers the source question. |
| 83–84 | Listener A · one minute of feedback. | One accurate observation + one next action for B. |
| 84–86 | Make the rehearsal improve the writing. | One final change based on the listener’s response. |
| 86–88 | Exit ticket · prove the skill. | A short written self-assessment with evidence from your paragraph. |
| 88–90 | Check the packet. Export. Keep it. | A final packet and an exported editable copy. |

## Unit 4 · Lesson 1 · Chart Detective: Read and Plan

Design: **Weather Fieldbook** — Olive field notes · contour maps · specimen labels.

| Minutes | Activity | Product |
|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | Read the landscape.Write the evidence. | A one-place evidence card, four labeled values and a 6–8-sentence first draft for TTD #5. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | I can identify graph labels, select accurate data, describe a supported pattern and avoid an invented forecast or cause. |
| 02–05 | Opinion or observation? | Two sentences with different evidence needs. |
| 05–07 | A number without a label is a puzzle. | A short data-label checklist. |
| 07–10 | Weather words need clear meanings. | Three meanings + a duration sentence. |
| 10–14 | Extreme weather · skim for topics. | Two main-idea decisions with clues. |
| 14–18 | Detail is not the same as a headline. | Two source-linked answers + one paraphrase. |
| 18–21 | A text example is not a current forecast. | A source-framed claim and a limit. |
| 21–23 | Watch or read · label the route. | One note labeled reading or video. |
| 23–25 | What is the graph actually measuring? | Two graph types and their units. |
| 25–27 | Teach the Document #5 · START HERE | One chosen-place graph description: four labeled values, 6–8 sentences, two revisions, a credited evidence card and a 3–5-minute lesson. |
| 27–29 | Your document is on the table. | A complete document passport: place, measures, period, units and page. |
| 29–31 | Annotate. Locate. Explain. | Two fully labeled observations + a source limitation. |
| 31–34 | Begin the assignment in class. | Four labeled evidence notes + one topic sentence. |
| 34–35 | What must your packet contain? | Circle what is already started. Put a star beside the next action for Lesson 2. |
| 35–37 | Average high / low · keep the series. | Two correct labels and one distinction. |
| 37–40 | Rise, fall and reach. | Two checked choices + a complete data sentence. |
| 40–43 | To / by / from…to. | Two sentences with correct meanings. |
| 43–45 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 45–47 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 47–50 | Plan the jobs before the sentences. | A one-place paragraph plan and four fully labeled source notes. |
| 50–52 | A partner tests the plan. | One precise suggestion and one decision recorded by the writer. |
| 52–53 | Set up a quiet writing sprint. | A visible plan, source, and a blank paragraph space. |
| 53–67 | Writing studio · 14 uninterrupted minutes. | A 6–8-sentence one-place draft with a traceable evidence card. |
| 67–68 | Peer review · agree on the reader’s job. | Partner names + the part each reader will verify. |
| 68–73 | Peer conference · evidence before advice. | One “keep” comment and one actionable “revise” comment per writer. |
| 73–77 | Revision 1 · improve the meaning. | Before → after → why, with an improved sentence in the paragraph. |
| 77–80 | Revision 2 · make the language accurate. | An edited sentence + a reason connected to this lesson’s language target. |
| 80–82 | Build the document you will point to. | A labeled visual draft with a source credit. |
| 82–85 | Teach a small part before the full talk. | Each partner gives a 60-second document explanation and receives 30 seconds of feedback. |
| 85–88 | Exit ticket · show the writing skill. | Three short responses: evidence, writing choice, next step. |
| 88–90 | Save today. Arrive ready next time. | Exported writing + a named action for the next lesson. |

## Unit 4 · Lesson 2 · Chart Detective: Describe and Teach

Design: **Desert Data Atlas** — Terracotta dunes · deep-blue instruments · sand panels.

| Minutes | Activity | Product |
|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | A day in the desert.A pattern in the data. | A precise one-place description, two explained revisions and a full TTD #5 teaching rehearsal. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | Leave with a written product, source evidence, a meaningful revision and a clear next step. |
| 02–05 | Retrieve a value with all its labels. | A complete evidence note. |
| 05–07 | One day and one year are different documents. | One time-scale distinction. |
| 07–10 | Predict, then verify. | Three meanings and one reading question. |
| 10–15 | Sahara · text and graph together. | Two graph-linked notes and two heading/paragraph matches. |
| 15–18 | Give the graph a complete caption. | A caption with accurate labels and a bounded claim. |
| 18–21 | Transfer the method, not the values. | An observation + relevant supporting value. |
| 21–23 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 23–25 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 25–28 | A topic sentence names the document. | An accurate topic sentence. |
| 28–31 | Relevant numbers support an observation. | Two matches + one supported observation. |
| 31–34 | Examples need natural sentence shapes. | A correctly framed data example. |
| 34–36 | Teach the Document #5 · reconnect. | A packet with the document, draft and revision evidence open. |
| 36–38 | Use the rubric as a revision tool. | One priority criterion and one sentence explaining the needed improvement. |
| 38–41 | Choose two changes before rewriting. | One meaning/evidence goal + one language goal. |
| 41–53 | Writing studio · 12 uninterrupted minutes. | A final one-place graph paragraph with four accurate labeled values. |
| 53–55 | Read it as someone new to the topic. | Two underlined strengths and one circled problem to discuss. |
| 55–56 | Before you exchange the paragraph… | A partner and a specific question for the reader. |
| 56–61 | Five-minute peer conference. | One located problem, one suggested repair and the writer’s decision. |
| 61–65 | Revise the content before polishing. | A meaningful before → after → reason revision receipt. |
| 65–68 | Edit the language that carries the meaning. | The final language revision receipt + corrected paragraph. |
| 68–70 | A visual should help a listener read. | A credited document with readable labels and selected annotations. |
| 70–73 | Build a 4-minute teaching route. | A cue outline, not a paragraph read aloud without explanation. |
| 73–74 | Partners A and B · set the stage. | Document visible, listener question ready, roles assigned. |
| 74–78 | TTD #5 · Partner A teaches. | A 4-minute explanation with a document-based listener check. |
| 78–79 | Listener B · one minute of feedback. | One accurate observation + one next action for A. |
| 79–83 | TTD #5 · Partner B teaches. | B’s 4-minute explanation; A answers the source question. |
| 83–84 | Listener A · one minute of feedback. | One accurate observation + one next action for B. |
| 84–86 | Make the rehearsal improve the writing. | One final change based on the listener’s response. |
| 86–88 | Exit ticket · prove the skill. | A short written self-assessment with evidence from your paragraph. |
| 88–90 | Check the packet. Export. Keep it. | A final packet and an exported editable copy. |

## Unit 4 · Lesson 3 · Weather Comparison: Compare and Draft

Design: **Polar Observatory** — Midnight blue · ice geometry · comparison instruments.

| Minutes | Activity | Product |
|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | Same month.Same measure.A fair comparison. | A matched-value comparison card and an 8–10-sentence first draft for TTD #6. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | I can compare both places and both measures, calculate a defensible difference and use accurate comparative language. |
| 02–05 | Say both sides of the comparison. | One traceable comparison. |
| 05–07 | A positive difference can cross zero. | A prediction to check later. |
| 07–10 | Comparative forms need a clear target. | A form bank + one complete comparison. |
| 10–14 | Read the two places as one document set. | One similarity and one fully labeled difference. |
| 14–18 | Superlatives need the full comparison set. | Two forms + one evidence rule. |
| 18–21 | Keep the practice source separate. | Two checked forms with source labels. |
| 21–23 | The final task has two dimensions. | A task checklist with both dimensions. |
| 23–25 | What does a fair pair share? | A fair pair + a justified rejection. |
| 25–27 | Teach the Document #6 · START HERE | A two-place comparison: both measures, four or more labeled values, 8–10 sentences, two revisions, a credited comparison visual and 3–5-minute teaching. |
| 27–29 | Your document is on the table. | Two fair comparison pairs with their source labels. |
| 29–31 | Annotate. Locate. Explain. | S / D / D / L annotations on the source or comparison card. |
| 31–34 | Begin the assignment in class. | A comparison plan and a source-labeled first support sentence. |
| 34–35 | What must your packet contain? | Circle what is already started. Put a star beside the next action for Lesson 4. |
| 35–37 | Run the comparison instrument. | A checked 26°C difference and a checked 25 mm difference. |
| 37–40 | Colder than · not more cold then. | A grammatical, bounded comparison. |
| 40–43 | Similarity is not exact equality. | A similarity and a difference. |
| 43–45 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 45–47 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 47–50 | Plan the jobs before the sentences. | A two-measure comparison plan with matched data. |
| 50–52 | A partner tests the plan. | One precise suggestion and one decision recorded by the writer. |
| 52–53 | Set up a quiet writing sprint. | A visible plan, source, and a blank paragraph space. |
| 53–67 | Writing studio · 14 uninterrupted minutes. | An 8–10-sentence two-place, two-measure first draft. |
| 67–68 | Peer review · agree on the reader’s job. | Partner names + the part each reader will verify. |
| 68–73 | Peer conference · evidence before advice. | One “keep” comment and one actionable “revise” comment per writer. |
| 73–77 | Revision 1 · improve the meaning. | Before → after → why, with an improved sentence in the paragraph. |
| 77–80 | Revision 2 · make the language accurate. | An edited sentence + a reason connected to this lesson’s language target. |
| 80–82 | Build the document you will point to. | A labeled visual draft with a source credit. |
| 82–85 | Teach a small part before the full talk. | Each partner gives a 60-second document explanation and receives 30 seconds of feedback. |
| 85–88 | Exit ticket · show the writing skill. | Three short responses: evidence, writing choice, next step. |
| 88–90 | Save today. Arrive ready next time. | Exported writing + a named action for the next lesson. |

Product: A defensible superlative or a cautious alternative.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A defensible superlative or a cautious alternative.


## Unit 4 · Lesson 4 · Weather Comparison: Revise and Publish

Design: **The Weather Exhibition** — Ivory gallery · forest-green labels · bronze details.

|---|---|---|
| 00–01 | Put the evidenceon display. | A polished two-place comparison, two revisions and a complete TTD #6 teaching rehearsal. |
| 01–02 | Your 90-minute route. | Leave with a written product, source evidence, a meaningful revision and a clear next step. |
| 02–05 | The whole paragraph in three lines. | A three-line comparison summary. |
| 05–07 | A gallery label must stand alone. | One missing-label action. |

| 10–15 | Audit every source value. | A source-checked evidence table. |
| 15–18 | Examples should clarify the comparison. | Two practice forms and one comparison example. |
| 18–21 | Apply the official task checklist. | A checklist diagnosis with a quoted sentence. |
| 21–23 | Model lab · opening and support | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 23–25 | Model lab · development and ending | A marked sentence job and a one-sentence explanation. |
| 25–28 | Repair form, measure and time. | Three reasoned corrections. |
| 28–31 | Use a superlative only when it is supported. | A verified superlative or a precise comparison. |
| 31–34 | The paragraph needs a readable route. | A revised sequence with a reason. |
| 34–36 | Teach the Document #6 · reconnect. | A packet with the document, draft and revision evidence open. |
| 36–38 | Use the rubric as a revision tool. | One priority criterion and one sentence explaining the needed improvement. |
| 38–41 | Choose two changes before rewriting. | One meaning/evidence goal + one language goal. |
| 41–53 | Writing studio · 12 uninterrupted minutes. | A final comparison meeting the pp.99–100 task and language checklists. |
| 53–55 | Read it as someone new to the topic. | Two underlined strengths and one circled problem to discuss. |
| 55–56 | Before you exchange the paragraph… | A partner and a specific question for the reader. |
| 56–61 | Five-minute peer conference. | One located problem, one suggested repair and the writer’s decision. |
| 61–65 | Revise the content before polishing. | A meaningful before → after → reason revision receipt. |
| 65–68 | Edit the language that carries the meaning. | The final language revision receipt + corrected paragraph. |
| 68–70 | A visual should help a listener read. | A credited document with readable labels and selected annotations. |
| 70–73 | Build a 4-minute teaching route. | A cue outline, not a paragraph read aloud without explanation. |
| 73–74 | Partners A and B · set the stage. | Document visible, listener question ready, roles assigned. |

| 84–86 | Make the rehearsal improve the writing. | One final change based on the listener’s response. |

Task: Ask a partner to sketch your selected comparison from the paragraph alone. Diagnose a missing label.

Product: A clarity test and repair.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A clarity test and repair.


**36 · Next-source question · optional, outside90 minutes**

Task: Write one useful question these graphs cannot answer and name the data needed.

Product: A question and an appropriate evidence plan.

Optional 5–8-minute challenge outside the 90-minute core. Use for fast finishers or a later class, not by taking time from independent writing. Output: A question and an appropriate evidence plan.
